# W-TEXT-UNIFORM-LINES: uniform, empty hard-break and single-line mixed-size advances

## Identity and scope

Latest continuation: [pagination, PDF downloads and current execution](#current-executable-handoff), September 12, 2026. This contract remains partial; the complete original pagination requirement remains active.

Word / S-TEXT; status **partial**. Bounded prerequisite within W-MODEL-SECTIONS, owning no complete ribbon command. Related controls are the line-spacing gallery and paragraph dialog. Native reference: installed x64 Word 16.0.4627.1000 with the Arial files hashed by `verify-word-line-metrics.ps1`.

This contract addresses line advances for homogeneous Latin text, empty paragraph marks and the bounded single-line mixed-size subset below. It does not certify exact mixed-run baselines or variable pagination. The parent contract remains partial and its automatic/minimum overflow gate stays closed.

## Behavioral specification

1. In `word-line-metrics.docx`, the wrapped homogeneous 10pt paragraph uses 1.5-line spacing. Word reports a 17.25pt advance. Chromium must use 23 CSSpx per line, with unchanged semantic multiplier 1.5 in saved content and DOCX.
2. In `word-font-cascade.docx`, the empty fourth paragraph has a 30pt paragraph mark and an 18pt minimum. Native caret-to-following-paragraph advance is 34.5pt; browser height must be 46 CSSpx, replacing the incorrect 24px minimum-only height.
3. Exact spacing retains its existing strut and fragment planner. Different font faces/weight/style, wrapped mixed sizes, inline objects, vertical text effects and complex scripts remain on their prior path; the single-line mixed-size extension is specified below.
4. Font loading invalidates cached metrics. Text edits/history schedule new measurements; composition defers them until compositionend. Model changes, undo records, content fingerprints and serialized HTML must not acquire measured pixel values or measurement attributes.
5. Existing spacing edits, empty-paragraph typing, undo/redo, reload, migration and actual DOCX export/reimport remain correct. Native script checks source browser advances separately from exported-file/native-PDF equality.

## Model and implementation

`word-font-line-metrics.ts` adds a WordEditor-only ProseMirror view plugin. A hidden neutral font probe measures browser normal-line leading at a large effective font size, cached by family/weight/style/stretch. Chromium caps very large requested sizes, so the measured height is divided by computed size, not requested size. No Arial ratio is hardcoded in runtime code. The 2355/2048 ratio in the unit test comes from the installed Arial GDI oracle.

The view applies twip-rounded automatic advances and a minimum floor through decorations and `mode-layout.css`. Stored paragraph spacing stays semantic. At most 5,000 paragraphs are considered per pass and the font cache is bounded. This is a conservative first font-metric path, not native performance parity or a general font/shaping engine. Missing paragraph-mark metrics, unsupported scripts and heterogeneous runs outside the bounded single-line extension are excluded. `word-surface-view.ts` continues to reject automatic/minimum overflow fragmentation.

## Acceptance matrix

| Dimension | Cases and evidence | Status |
| --- | --- | --- |
| import/render | Existing two native fixtures; 17.25pt automatic and 34.5pt empty-minimum advances | Passing targeted Chromium cases |
| edit/persist | Existing spacing/typing/history/reload/migration workflows | Targeted regressions pass |
| export | Semantic spacing retained; actual downloads independently reopened in Word | Native comparison required after each browser run |
| native | Script compares measured source advances; saved-file property and PDF comparisons are separate | Bound to current local receipts |
| create | Authored explicit regular-font exact pagination, history/reload, DOCX and PDF output now have bounded native evidence; default/automatic/minimum and paragraph-mark semantics remain required | Partial; see current executable handoff |
| interaction/errors | Composition scheduling and unsupported-flow fallback implemented; full IME/caret/baseline and native UI matrix | Pending |
| performance | Cache and traversal bounds implemented; sustained large-model benchmark | Pending |

Further requirements: mixed-run ascent/descent and terminal-mark contribution, per-line baseline measurements, multiple fonts/scripts/browser engines, caret/selection/printing, and the variable-line planner. Do not treat matching these two line advances as completion of pagination.

## Single-line mixed-size subset

September 10, 2026. The new authored native fixture `tests/fixtures/word-mixed-lines.docx` contains six single-line Arial paragraphs, each with 10pt / 30pt / 10pt text. It varies Single/1.5-line/minimum-18pt/minimum-50pt spacing and 10pt/40pt terminal paragraph marks, followed by a sentinel paragraph. `native-word-mixed-lines.json` binds the Word-saved source, installed executable and Arial hashes. The generator's original script hash is historical; later native comparison receipts bind the current comparison script separately. No user document content or font binaries are distributed.

Observed Word line advances are 34.5, 51.75, 34.5, 34.5, approximately 49.9 and 34.5 points. The 40pt terminal mark does **not** enlarge these nonempty lines. The empty 30pt mark case remains covered by the earlier fixture. Native PDF baseline extraction shows additional automatic leading below the text and minimum leading above it in this installed build. This is measured implementation behavior, not a claim that generic CSS or every Office build uses that rule.

The browser previously produced a 30pt line for the first case. It also added a 16px paragraph margin where Word had omitted zero spacing. The new measurement path uses the existing font probe to calculate each run's natural leading, adds the measured automatic/minimum excess on the appropriate side, and suppresses redundant normal struts from formatting wrappers. Imported source paragraphs now default to zero vertical margins; explicit/inherited margins remain inline and take precedence. This also corrects older saved imported documents without changing their content or revision. See [Microsoft's spacing implementation notes](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/openspecs/office_standards/ms-oi29500/264a0139-8174-4574-b20d-ab778e770931) and [spacing options](https://support.microsoft.com/en-us/word/adjust-indents-and-spacing), researched September 10. Auto-spacing and line-unit paragraph spacing remain separate unimplemented cases.

Eligibility is intentionally bounded: Latin text, a single visual line, one font family/weight/style/stretch, baseline-aligned text only, explicit imported paragraph-font metadata, no hard breaks or inline objects, automatic multiples at least 1, and resolved semantic point/pixel run sizes. The original 1.5-times paragraph-mark exclusion was removed by the large-mark subset below. Wrapped text, mixed faces, vertical effects and complex scripts retain the prior path. Width changes trigger a new measurement; shrinking the editor removes the decoration, and restoring width restores it. Measurements remain ProseMirror view decorations and never enter saved content or undo history.

Acceptance covers six actual paragraph-to-paragraph advances as well as box heights, selecting `Large` and changing 30pt to 20pt, minimum-spacing edits, invalid zero input, Undo/Redo, saved reload, actual DOCX downloads, reimport and absence of measurement metadata in IndexedDB. Comparison tolerance for native COM advances is 0.15pt; the 50pt minimum is reported as approximately 49.9pt by the native layout oracle, while CSS represents 50pt to subpixel precision. Native exported-file comparison is exact for character text/font/size/page/x/y and paragraph spacing properties, with independent Word edits and separate exact PDF comparison.

The first authored paragraph contains Word's hidden `_GoBack` bookmark. Its formatting export still rejects under the existing preservation guard. Editing acceptance therefore targets the second paragraph. This is an explicit remaining formatting limitation, not silently removed fixture metadata. Precise browser baseline matching remains open: the diagnostic PDF matrix comparison records the remaining fractions of a point instead of declaring browser glyph parity from correct heights.

```powershell
# Deliberate regeneration only; then copy source.docx/source-report.json to fixtures.
node scripts/word-mixed-lines-fixture.mjs
powershell.exe -NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File scripts/verify-word-mixed-lines.ps1 -Create

# Ordinary verification against the checked source fixture:
npx.cmd vitest run src/word-font-line-metrics.test.ts
npx.cmd playwright test tests/word-mixed-lines.spec.ts tests/word-line-metrics.spec.ts --workers=1
powershell.exe -NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File scripts/verify-word-mixed-lines.ps1 -Compare
python scripts/compare-word-mixed-lines-pdfs.py
```

Run native automation outside the sandbox. It checks the pinned executable, requires an idle owned Word instance, disables macros/link updates, saves only authored copies and restores its preferences. Local source/browser/native/PDF evidence lives under `.local/word-mixed-lines`.

## Large paragraph mark subset

September 10, 2026. Nonempty single-line Latin runs with one font descriptor now exclude the paragraph-mark strut regardless of its relative size. The view reads point/pixel sizes from semantic ProseMirror marks and paragraph attributes, sets the decorated paragraph's font size to zero, and explicitly restores each run's semantic size in its decoration. This prevents the mark's baseline offset from enlarging the line. It also prevents the measured zero or a prior decoration's font size from becoming the next measurement after an edit. Clearing the text removes the mixed-run decoration and restores the empty paragraph's original mark metrics; no model/font/storage value becomes zero.

The authored 18-paragraph fixture adds three native source cases with a 40pt mark and 8pt text mixed with 10/20/30pt runs. Their native 1.5-line advances are 17.25/34.5/51.75pt. The first case previously rendered nearly 60pt in Chromium, a difference of approximately 42.74pt. The new browser workflow changes the 10pt run to 20pt, clears the paragraph and types `New` into the empty 40pt paragraph. Both empty and typed lines render at 69pt. Each stage supports Undo/Redo, saved reload, actual DOCX download and reimport. Independent native Word edits match all three exports in character text/fonts/sizes/coordinates and paragraph spacing; all three rendered PDF pages match exactly at 144 DPI.

This is an extension of the existing line-advance contract, owning no complete command. Wrapped runs, mixed font faces/scripts and exact glyph baselines remain open. The repeated native baseline, browser normal/canvas, XPS and exploratory printer measurements are documented in [WORD_BASELINE_RESEARCH.md](../WORD_BASELINE_RESEARCH.md). They do not justify a universal baseline offset. Local XPS output includes embedded fonts and is not part of the distributed fixtures.

```powershell
npx.cmd vitest run src/word-font-line-metrics.test.ts
npx.cmd playwright test tests/word-paragraph-mark.spec.ts tests/word-mixed-lines.spec.ts tests/word-line-metrics.spec.ts --workers=1
powershell.exe -NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File scripts/verify-word-mixed-lines.ps1 -Probe -Compare
python scripts/compare-word-mixed-lines-pdfs.py --probe
python scripts/report-word-baselines.py
```

The original checked native fixture receipt binds source/executable/font data; current native reports bind later script changes and actual browser outputs. Run native automation outside the sandbox using an idle owned instance. Current artifacts are under `.local/word-baselines`.

## Uniform single-line leading placement

September 10, 2026. Uniform nonempty text now uses the existing single-line natural-height and excess-leading model even when every run has the paragraph mark's font size. Previously, the homogeneous branch set only the paragraph line height, letting CSS center extra leading. An independent native test on paragraph 10 of `word-baselines.docx` showed that changing Arial 10pt text from 1.5-line to Double must not shift its glyphs within the paragraph. The old browser implementation shifted them by 2.25pt; the new regression failed before the fix.

`word-font-line-metrics.ts` now evaluates single-line eligibility for uniform and mixed-size text alike. Natural per-run leading is retained; automatic excess follows the text and minimum excess precedes it. Homogeneous wrapped text and empty marks retain their previous advance path. Mixed wrapped text still falls back. No semantic attributes, stored fonts, history entries or exported measurements are introduced. The full browser suite then exposed stale section measurements: font decorations update after an edit, and absolutely positioned paragraph growth need not resize the editor. `word-surface-view.ts` now observes `wordFontLineMetricsKey` state changes and schedules fresh surface measurements. The existing 700-repeat long-text fallback and Undo recovery regression failed before this correction and passes afterward. Exact spacing, unsupported scripts/faces/effects and paragraph-flow gates remain unchanged.

The new `tests/word-uniform-leading.spec.ts` exercises Double, a 50pt minimum and Single on the same 10pt paragraph, plus Undo/Redo at each stage, invalid zero minimum, saved reload, actual DOCX downloads/reimport and narrow-width fallback/recovery. The original 10/20/30pt source advances remain checked by the native harness. `tests/fixtures/native-word-uniform-leading.json` records independently saved native edits from the existing authored source before the browser fix. The source hash remains `431950979466304232e873a4a430fe55e85da807d470e4a9904c05b93cc562b2`.

All three browser downloads match native text/font/size/position/spacing snapshots exactly and have identical native-rendered PDF pixels at 144 DPI. A separate comparison subtracts the source-relative glyph position from each stage: native Double and Single deltas are 0pt; the 50pt minimum delta is 38.5200pt. Browser deltas are 0pt and 38.4961pt respectively, within the existing 0.15pt coordinate tolerance. The browser uses the first character's DOM range top; native uses its PDF baseline minus COM line origin. Because the font and text are identical across these edits, their differences measure leading placement; they do not equate those absolute coordinate APIs or certify glyph baselines. Empty paragraphs, wrapped lines, other faces/scripts and full typography still need broader placement acceptance.

```powershell
# Deliberate native expectation regeneration, after staging the checked source:
powershell.exe -NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File scripts/verify-word-mixed-lines.ps1 -UniformLeading -Reference
# Ordinary workflow, actual browser files before native comparison:
npx.cmd playwright test tests/word-uniform-leading.spec.ts --workers=1
powershell.exe -NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File scripts/verify-word-mixed-lines.ps1 -UniformLeading -Compare
python scripts/compare-word-mixed-lines-pdfs.py --uniform-leading
```

The native harness uses the pinned executable, an idle owned instance, macros/link updates disabled and restored preferences. All copies live under `.local/word-uniform-leading`; no user document is edited. Relative leading placement and native-to-native export pixels have separate report fields. The exact-baseline diagnostic is still open.

## Uniform wrapped-line subset

September 10, 2026. `word-wrapped-leading.docx` reduces the existing 35-word Arial 10pt case to one authored page, alongside three explicit hard-break lines and two sentinels. The source is saved in pinned Word, its author/editor metadata is changed to Noffice without rewriting XML namespaces, and native expectations are regenerated before browser implementation. Source SHA-256: `a993fb39fef480f446a380377fde8fdd7074d07f041812b5a4f4a76dfaed9aa9`. The fixture and `native-word-wrapped-leading.json` contain no redistributed fonts or user document text.

The first test failed native line identities: the browser moved `word7` to line two. Inspection found a 278px text rectangle instead of the authored 280px and inherited `white-space: break-spaces`. The source-page border now uses an outline outside layout, with full source-height sizing and no printed outline. Imported paragraphs use `pre-wrap`, preserving whitespace while permitting trailing wrap-spaces to hang. The tested 210pt body width and all seven native soft-wrap boundaries now match; the hard-break paragraph retains all three lines.

For homogeneous Latin runs with the same descriptor and size as their paragraph mark, `word-font-line-metrics.ts` uses natural-height inline decorations aligned to each CSS line box's top for automatic multiples and bottom for minimum spacing. The paragraph retains its measured line advance. Native text nodes, hard breaks and selection remain owned by ProseMirror; no synthetic lines or model/history/font changes are introduced. The view recognizes its own alignment decoration when checking supported vertical effects. Mixed fonts/sizes, complex scripts, explicit leading/trailing/consecutive empty hard-break lines and exact-spacing paths remain outside this extension.

`tests/word-wrapped-leading.spec.ts` checks all seven wrapped and three hard-break identities against native source expectations; Double, 18pt minimum and Single on both paragraphs; invalid zero input; Undo/Redo for each edit; saved reload; actual downloads and reimport; 50% zoom, pointer hit testing and typing/Undo on the second hard-break line; and absence of metric decorations in IndexedDB. The initial whole-paragraph 0.05pt browser tolerance was inappropriate at 50% zoom: seven rounded line boxes accumulated 0.0546875pt. The test now bounds each line by one 1/64 CSSpx layout unit, normalized for zoom, rather than accepting an arbitrary larger error. This is separate from the unchanged 0.15pt native coordinate tolerance and exact native PDF pixel gate. [Chromium layout-unit representation](https://chromium.googlesource.com/chromium/src/+/96b04b8744c3300c980cd87bc41cc3444d463fb0/third_party/blink/renderer/platform/geometry/layout_unit.h), [CSS inline line boxes and leading](https://www.w3.org/TR/CSS22/visudet.html#leading).

Native comparison covers exact character/font/position/spacing snapshots and exact native-rendered PDFs for all three actual browser exports. Eight source/stage paragraph comparisons cover 40 line identities. The first soft-wrapped line has a fixed preceding paragraph across edits: native Double/Single glyph shifts are 0pt and minimum shift is 6.47998pt; browser shifts are 0pt and 6.50391pt, within 0.15pt. These three first-line comparisons are explicitly separated from the other 27 coordinate diagnostics. Four of those diagnostics, on the later hard-break paragraph, exceed 0.15pt (maximum approximately 0.288pt). They remain open; the report's passing subset flag does not certify those coordinates, absolute baselines or variable pagination. Native source advances are **34.5, 34.5, 51.75, 51.75, 34.5, 34.5, 86.25, 51.75 and 103.5pt**. A terminated empty line uses the break's font; an empty final line uses the paragraph-mark font. ## Paragraph-coordinate and mixed-wrap continuation

September 11, 2026. This contract and all parent families remain **partial**. The original four mixed-coordinate diagnostics are preserved, with the unchanged 0.15pt threshold. They subtracted a COM character/range origin from a PDF glyph coordinate, then compared that result with a browser paragraph-box offset. The new native paragraph-background probe establishes rendered paragraph origins independently. In the uniform fixture, all glyph matrices remain exactly unchanged across the unshaded control, shaded measurement and original native PDF. The comparable acceptance checks use per-state line advances and first-line leading relative to that measured paragraph background; absolute browser glyph baselines are still open. No constant font offset or wider threshold is introduced. [Word's range Information API](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/api/microsoft.office.interop.word.wdinformation?view=word-pia) describes a range/selection position, which must not be assumed to equal the rendered paragraph box.

The new `word-mixed-wrap.docx` and `native-word-mixed-wrap.json` contain MIT-authored Arial 10/20/30pt words, 10/40pt paragraph marks and sentinels. Independent native source, Double, 50pt minimum, Single, a 30-to-20pt word edit and caret typing produce six alpha lines and five beta lines per state. Source paragraph heights are approximately 258.740pt and 224.184pt. The font edit retains a 30pt space but reduces the nonempty line's advance; browser text widths must retain that space without letting its font set the line height.

The view implementation now measures bounded same-descriptor Latin soft lines, preserves the original text/caret and inserts a noneditable, aria-hidden zero-width strut after each line's first word. Placing it before that word incorrectly allowed it to remain on the preceding line. Semantic font sizes are restored through inline decorations; the per-line strut owns vertical layout so separately rounded inline boxes cannot accumulate zoom error. Spaces retain their font/width without contributing height. A second measurement settles mapped font/line decorations after edits even when the fixed page height hides a resize. Work is bounded to 4,096 characters per eligible paragraph, 10,000 per pass and fewer than eight settling passes. Unsupported objects, descriptor changes, mixed hard breaks, complex scripts, intraword splits and excessive work retain fallback. No view attributes, pixels, widgets or synthetic line breaks enter storage or DOCX.

The mixed fixture's background probe preserves every measured vertical glyph coordinate exactly. Shading changes horizontal PDF painting quantization in a reduced case (about 0.108pt); full matrix equality is therefore **not** claimed for that fixture. Paragraph-height and line-identity acceptance uses the independent vertical evidence only. Browser absolute/relative glyph-baseline differences remain explicit diagnostics in the mixed-wrap PDF report; these are not certified by passing paragraph heights or exported-file pixels.

Native Save merged two adjacent plain font runs in paragraph 3 (14 source runs became 13), while retained export kept the original fragment. The remaining typing case required preserving the inserted text's run boundary and native save-session identity: the native copy contains `alpha`, a separately identified inserted `0`, and `1 alpha2 `. Eligible plain insertions now retain those boundaries and lazily register a new save ID in an existing native settings register. The ID is above existing valid IDs and reused within that export; malformed/exhausted/missing registers are preserved. No author identity or tracked-change markup is invented. ## Bounded native glyph-baseline continuation

September 11, 2026; this non-ribbon line-metric contract remains **partial**. The current implementation corrects the tested regular Arial mixed-size soft-line baselines using a native font/spacing profile. A new independent fixture measures seven font sizes (10/15/20/25/30/35/40pt) in Single, 1.5, Double and 50pt minimum spacing, for 28 native paragraph cases. The existing independent mixed-wrap fixture remains unchanged. Its 20pt Single native placement ranges from 18.474 to 18.600pt across paragraph contexts; the profile uses the twip-rounded midpoint 18.55pt. No browser result was used to change the native oracle or the existing 0.15pt acceptance bound.

The investigation first reproduced the glyph differences in actual Chromium PDFs, distinguishing them from DOM-probe error. A high-resolution GDI descender prediction fit the initial 14 font cases but failed later spacing/context cases. Expanding the independent calibration to 28 cases established the measured spacing-specific profile; the earlier prediction remains diagnostic. Native PDF font-size quantization (for example 20.04pt for authored 20pt) is observable, so equal glyph outlines or complete printer emulation are not claimed. [CSS inline font metrics](https://www.w3.org/TR/css-inline-3/), [OpenType OS/2 metrics](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/typography/opentype/spec/os2) and [VDMX hinted bounds](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/typography/opentype/otspec184/vdmx) describe relevant metric distinctions; they do not specify Word's measured spacing behavior.

`src/word-native-baseline.ts` accepts only the measured regular Arial descriptor, effective normal-height ratio, seven sizes and four spacing states. Unsupported sizes, faces, weights, styles, stretches, metrics and spacing return null. The view enables the profile only when every measured soft line qualifies; otherwise the existing bounded line-box path remains. Family/ratio matching is not cryptographic font identity: other system font versions, fallback resolution, operating systems and printer contexts still need evidence. The 28 calibration cases test the pure profile; the complete browser/native editing matrix currently exercises the existing 10/20/30pt mixed fixture, not all 28 isolated paragraphs as interactive documents.

`src/word-font-line-metrics.ts` removes the previous view shift before grouping text by logical baseline, then derives each target baseline from the measured paragraph origin, line advances and native ascent. A compensating relative position and transform preserve the logical glyph/caret position while avoiding independently rounded fractional inline paint origins. The paragraph preserves its fractional paint origin as well. The line strut stays outside the transformed word. **Exact next implementation.** Eight native inline-break fixtures are already captured and promoted: `tests/fixtures/word-inline-flow-{page-midline,column-midline,column-single,page-columns,leading-column,terminal-column,consecutive-column,terminal-page}.docx` and `native-word-inline-flow.json`. Helpers `.local/prepare-inline-flow.py`, `.local/probe-inline-flow.ps1`, `.local/promote-inline-flow.py` and artifacts `.local/word-inline-flow/` bind the independent source, saved DOCX, PDF, font, executable, character positions and terminal caret. They are oracles only; inline column support and inline-break pagination have **not** been implemented yet.

The measured rules are: page breaks advance a physical page, including inside two columns; column breaks advance the column and then the page (a one-column section therefore advances a page). Leading/consecutive breaks consume columns. The break control belongs to the preceding line (`\f` for page, U+000E for column); terminal breaks place the real paragraph caret in the next column/page, including an otherwise blank final page. Start with the existing `src/word-page-break.ts` atom, add a column counterpart without changing old page-break identities, preserve/import/export both controls (`docx-import.ts`, `docx-preserve.ts`, `formats.ts`), and extend measured semantic line ranges plus `planWordFragments` with forced page/column boundaries. The surface input currently rejects those atoms; its short plan must not swallow a forced break. Compose the real terminal caret anchor in both X and Y. The ephemeral print tree must slice each semantic fragment once, and must not let an old block-styled break or duplicated hidden paragraph inject extra PDF content. `docx` already provides `ColumnBreak`; no dependency is needed.

Then verify creation/import, real pointer/keyboard editing, deletion, history/reload/migration, original preservation, invalid/unsupported/transaction recovery, actual exports/native re-edits/reimport and terminal-page printing for all eight cases. Keep remaining keep-with-next chains (including oversized followers), unequal columns, separators, `nextColumn` section starts, complete section authoring, headers/footers, broader fonts/absolute glyph baselines, empty/mixed lines and the full selection/failure/performance/accessibility/browser matrix open. New document pagination without retained section metadata is another required path; do not assume the imported-source flow certifies creation. Continue this checkbox rather than switching applications.
